Here's what I found and how to improve your setup:

## What You Have Installed

### Windows
- **Windows 11 Home** (Build 26200) — installed Jan 19, 2026
- **Lenovo 81BD** (x64)

### Development Tools (all working)
- **Python 3.14.5** — very latest, may have package compatibility issues
- **Node v24.18.0** — latest, but ecosystem may lag behind
- **npm 11.16.0**
- **Go 1.26.0**
- **Git 2.53.0**
- **Docker Desktop** — installed but **not running**
- **VS Code** with **73 extensions**
- **WSL** with **Ubuntu** (default v2) — **currently stopped**

### Fedora / Linux
- **No Fedora installed** — neither as WSL distro nor VM
- Only Linux environment is **WSL Ubuntu** (stopped)

---

## Improvements

### 1. WSL — Start Ubuntu or Add Fedora
Your Ubuntu distro is stopped. Either start it, or if you specifically want Fedora:

```bash
# Option A: Start existing Ubuntu
wsl --start Ubuntu

# Option B: Install Fedora on WSL
wsl --install -d Fedora
```

After install, verify with:
```bash
wsl --list --verbose
```

### 2. Docker Desktop — Start the Daemon
Docker is installed but the engine isn't running. Start it from the system tray, then verify:
```bash
docker --version
docker run hello-world
```

### 3. Python Version Strategy
Python 3.14.5 is brand-new. Some packages (especially scientific/ML) may not have wheels yet. Consider:
- Keeping 3.14 for general use
- Adding a **Python 3.11** or **3.12** environment for compatibility with older packages (you already have 3.11.15 in your Hermes venv)
- Use `pyenv-win` to manage multiple Python versions:
```bash
# via winget
winget install pyenv-win
```

### 4. Node.js Version Management
Node 24 is very new. If you hit package compatibility issues, use `nvm-windows` to switch versions:
```bash
winget install CoreyButler.NVMforWindows
nvm list available
nvm install 20
nvm use 20
```

### 5. VS Code Extensions Audit
73 extensions is a lot. Clean up unused ones:
```bash
code --list-extensions | wc -l   # shows count
# In VS Code: Extensions view → sort by "Enabled" → disable/remove unused
```

Look for duplicates (e.g., multiple Python/Prettier variants) and old versions.

### 6. Windows 11 Home Limitation
Windows 11 Home **cannot run Hyper-V** or WSL2 with full performance in some configurations. If you need proper Linux VMs:
- Upgrade to **Windows 11 Pro** (~$99 upgrade), or
- Use **VirtualBox** / **VMware Workstation Player** (free) for full VM support

### 7. Package Manager Setup
Ensure you have at least one package manager:
```bash
winget --version  # usually preinstalled on Win 11
choco --version   # if not, install from chocolatey.org
```

### 8. Quick Win — Auto-start Docker + WSL on Boot
Since you use both frequently:
- **Docker Desktop**: Settings → General → "Start Docker Desktop when you log in"
- **WSL**: Create a startup script or use Task Scheduler to run `wsl --start Ubuntu`

---

### Summary Priority Order
| Priority | Action | Why |
|---|---|---|
| **High** | Start Docker Desktop | Needed for container/DevOps work |
| **High** | Start/install WSL distro | Linux tools require a running distro |
| **Medium** | Install pyenv-win | Python 3.14 may break older packages |
| **Medium** | Audit VS Code extensions | 73 extensions = slow startup, bloat |
| **Low** | Install Fedora via WSL | If you specifically need Fedora tools |
| **Low** | Upgrade to Win 11 Pro | Only if you need Hyper-V/advanced VMs |

Based on what I can see, here's the situation:

## Your Drives
| Partition | Size | Mounted As | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| sda3 | ~387GB | C: (Windows 11) | Windows OS |
| sda5 | ~2TB | **not mounted** | Likely your Fedora drive |
| sdb1 | ~15GB | E: | Has Windows boot files (bootmgr, efi, sources) — likely a recovery/install USB |

## What This Means
You have a **dual-boot setup**:
- **C:** = Windows 11 Home (Build 26200)
- **sda5** = Fedora (the 2TB partition you boot into separately)

Since sda5 can't be mounted from Windows/bash, I can't inspect Fedora's details directly. But here's how to improve both sides:

---

## Fedora Improvements (sda5)

### 1. Update Fedora
```bash
# On Fedora
sudo dnf update -y
sudo dnf upgrade --refresh -y
```

### 2. Install Essential Dev Tools
```bash
sudo dnf groupinstall -y "Development Tools" "Development Libraries"
sudo dnf install -y git vim tmux htop curl wget
```

### 3. Set Up Python on Fedora
```bash
sudo dnf install -y python3 python3-pip python3-venv
# Or use pyenv for version management
curl https://pyenv.run | bash
```

### 4. Set Up Node.js on Fedora
```bash
# Use NodeSource or nvm
curl -fsSL https://rpm.nodesource.com/setup_22.x | sudo bash -
sudo dnf install -y nodejs
```

### 5. Docker on Fedora
```bash
sudo dnf install -y docker-compose
sudo systemctl enable --now docker
sudo usermod -aG docker $USER
```

### 6. VS Code on Fedora
```bash
sudo rpm --import https://packages.microsoft.com/keys/microsoft.asc
sudo dnf config-manager --add-repo https://packages.microsoft.com/yumrepos/vscode
sudo dnf install -y code
```

---

## Windows Improvements

### 1. Boot Manager — Add Fedora to Windows Boot Menu
You're likely using BIOS boot menu to switch. To add Fedora to the Windows boot manager:

1. Boot into **Fedora**, install `efibootmgr` if not present
2. Note the boot entry number for Fedora
3. Boot into **Windows**, run:
```powershell
bcdedit /set {bootmgr} path \EFI\fedora\shimx64.efi
```

### 2. Share Files Between OSes
Create a shared **NTFS partition** (or use the existing sda5 if it has free space):
```bash
# On Fedora - mount Windows C: drive
sudo mount -t ntfs-3g /dev/sda3 /mnt/windows
```

Or set up **Samba** for network sharing between the two OSes.

### 3. Enable WSL2 (if you need Linux tools in Windows)
Your WSL Ubuntu is installed but stopped. Start it when you need Linux tools without rebooting:
```bash
wsl --start Ubuntu
```

### 4. Optimize Dual Boot
- Disable **Fast Startup** in Windows (Control Panel → Power Options) — it can cause filesystem corruption on shared NTFS drives
- Use the same **timezone** on both OSes to avoid timestamp confusion

---

## Quick Wins Priority

| Priority | Action |
|---|---|
| **High** | Boot Fedora, run `sudo dnf update` |
| **High** | Disable Windows Fast Startup |
| **Medium** | Set up shared NTFS partition for file exchange |
| **Medium** | Install Docker + VS Code on Fedora |
| **Low** | Add Fedora to Windows boot manager (optional) |
| **Low** | Set up Samba sharing between OSes |

---

If you want me to help with specific Fedora configs, boot me into Fedora and share:
```bash
cat /etc/os-release
sudo fdisk -l
df -h
```
